# Skill Creator

A skill for creating new skills and iteratively improving them.

At a high level, the process of creating a skill goes like this:

- Decide what you want the skill to do and roughly how it should do it
- Write a draft of the skill
- Create a few test prompts and run claude-with-access-to-the-skill on them
- Help the user evaluate the results both qualitatively and quantitatively
  - While the runs happen in the background, draft some quantitative evals if there aren't any (if there are some, you can either use as is or modify if you feel something needs to change about them). Then explain them to the user (or if they already existed, explain the ones that already exist)
  - Use the `eval-viewer/generate_review.py` script to show the user the results for them to look at, and also let them look at the quantitative metrics
- Rewrite the skill based on feedback from the user's evaluation of the results (and also if there are any glaring flaws that become apparent from the quantitative benchmarks)
- Repeat until you're satisfied
- Expand the test set and try again at larger scale

Your job when using this skill is to figure out where the user is in this process and then jump in and help them progress through these stages. So for instance, maybe they're like "I want to make a skill for X". You can help narrow down what they mean, write a draft, write the test cases, figure out how they want to evaluate, run all the prompts, and repeat.

On the other hand, maybe they already have a draft of the skill. In this case you can go straight to the eval/iterate part of the loop.

Of course, you should always be flexible and if the user is like "I don't need to run a bunch of evaluations, just vibe with me", you can do that instead.

Then after the skill is done (but again, the order is flexible), you can also run the skill description improver, which we have a whole separate script for, to optimize the triggering of the skill.

Cool? Cool.

## Communicating with the user

The skill creator is liable to be used by people across a wide range of familiarity with coding jargon. If you haven't heard (and how could you, it's only very recently that it started), there's a trend now where the power of Claude is inspiring plumbers to open up their terminals, parents and grandparents to google "how to install npm". On the other hand, the bulk of users are probably fairly computer-literate.

So please pay attention to context cues to understand how to phrase your communication! In the default case, just to give you some idea:

- "evaluation" and "benchmark" are borderline, but OK
- for "JSON" and "assertion" you want to see serious cues from the user that they know what those things are before using them without explaining them

It's OK to briefly explain terms if you're in doubt, and feel free to clarify terms with a short definition if you're unsure if the user will get it.

---

## Creating a skill

### Capture Intent

Start by understanding the user's intent. The current conversation might already contain a workflow the user wants to capture (e.g., they say "turn this into a skill"). If so, extract answers from the conversation history first — the tools used, the sequence of steps, corrections the user made, input/output formats observed. The user may need to fill the gaps, and should confirm before proceeding to the next step.

1. What should this skill enable Claude to do?
2. When should this skill trigger? (what user phrases/contexts)
3. What's the expected output format?
4. Should we set up test cases to verify the skill works? Skills with objectively verifiable outputs (file transforms, data extraction, code generation, fixed workflow steps) benefit from test cases. Skills with subjective outputs (writing style, art) often don't need them. Suggest the appropriate default based on the skill type, but let the user decide.

### Interview and Research

Proactively ask questions about edge cases, input/output formats, example files, success criteria, and dependencies. Wait to write test prompts until you've got this part ironed out.

Check available MCPs - if useful for research (searching docs, finding similar skills, looking up best practices), research in parallel via subagents if available, otherwise inline. Come prepared with context to reduce burden on the user.

### Write the SKILL.md

Based on the user interview, fill in these components:

- **name**: Skill identifier
- **description**: When to trigger, what it does. This is the primary triggering mechanism - include both what the skill does AND specific contexts for when to use it. All "when to use" info goes here, not in the body. Note: currently Claude has a tendency to "undertrigger" skills -- to not use them when they'd be useful. To combat this, please make the skill descriptions a little bit "pushy". So for instance, instead of "How to build a simple fast dashboard to display internal Anthropic data.", you might write "How to build a simple fast dashboard to display internal Anthropic data. Make sure to use this skill whenever the user mentions dashboards, data visualization, internal metrics, or wants to display any kind of company data, even if they don't explicitly ask for a 'dashboard.'"
- **compatibility**: Required tools, dependencies (optional, rarely needed)
- **the rest of the skill :)**

### Skill Writing Guide

#### Anatomy of a Skill

```
skill-name/
├── SKILL.md (required)
│   ├── YAML frontmatter (name, description required)
│   └── Markdown instructions
└── Bundled Resources (optional)
    ├── scripts/    - Executable code for deterministic/repetitive tasks
    ├── references/ - Docs loaded into context as needed
    └── assets/     - Files used in output (templates, icons, fonts)
```

#### Progressive Disclosure

Skills use a three-level loading system:
1. **Metadata** (name + description) - Always in context (~100 words)
2. **SKILL.md body** - In context whenever skill triggers (<500 lines ideal)
3. **Bundled resources** - As needed (unlimited, scripts can execute without loading)

These word counts are approximate and you can feel free to go longer if needed.

**Key patterns:**
- Keep SKILL.md under 500 lines; if you're approaching this limit, add an additional layer of hierarchy along with clear pointers about where the model using the skill should go next to follow up.
- Reference files clearly from SKILL.md with guidance on when to read them
- For large reference files (>300 lines), include a table of contents

**Domain organization**: When a skill supports multiple domains/frameworks, organize by variant:
```
cloud-deploy/
├── SKILL.md (workflow + selection)
└── references/
    ├── aws.md
    ├── gcp.md
    └── azure.md
```
Claude reads only the relevant reference file.

#### Principle of Lack of Surprise

This goes without saying, but skills must not contain malware, exploit code, or any content that could compromise system security. A skill's contents should not surprise the user in their intent if described. Don't go along with requests to create misleading skills or skills designed to facilitate unauthorized access, data exfiltration, or other malicious activities. Things like a "roleplay as an XYZ" are OK though.

#### Writing Patterns

Prefer using the imperative form in instructions.

**Defining output formats** - You can do it like this:
```markdown
## Report structure
ALWAYS use this exact template:
# [Title]
## Executive summary
## Key findings
## Recommendations
```

**Examples pattern** - It's useful to include examples. You can format them like this (but if "Input" and "Output" are in the examples you might want to deviate a little):
```markdown
## Commit message format
**Example 1:**
Input: Added user authentication with JWT tokens
Output: feat(auth): implement JWT-based authentication
```

### Writing Style

Try to explain to the model why things are important in lieu of heavy-handed musty MUSTs. Use theory of mind and try to make the skill general and not super-narrow to specific examples. Start by writing a draft and then look at it with fresh eyes and improve it.

### Test Cases

After writing the skill draft, come up with 2-3 realistic test prompts — the kind of thing a real user would actually say. Share them with the user: [you don't have to use this exact language] "Here are a few test cases I'd like to try. Do these look right, or do you want to add more?" Then run them.

Save test cases to `evals/evals.json`. Don't write assertions yet — just the prompts. You'll draft assertions in the next step while the runs are in progress.

```json
{
  "skill_name": "example-skill",
  "evals": [
    {
      "id": 1,
      "prompt": "User's task prompt",
      "expected_output": "Description of expected result",
      "files": []
    }
  ]
}
```

See `references/schemas.md` for the full schema (including the `assertions` field, which you'll add later).

## Running and evaluating test cases

This section is one continuous sequence — don't stop partway through. Do NOT use `/skill-test` or any other testing skill.

Put results in `<skill-name>-workspace/` as a sibling to the skill directory. Within the workspace, organize results by iteration (`iteration-1/`, `iteration-2/`, etc.) and within that, each test case gets a directory (`eval-0/`, `eval-1/`, etc.). Don't create all of this upfront — just create directories as you go.

### Step 1: Spawn all runs (with-skill AND baseline) in the same turn

For each test case, spawn two subagents in the same turn — one with the skill, one without. This is important: don't spawn the with-skill runs first and then come back for baselines later. Launch everything at once so it all finishes around the same time.

**With-skill run:**

```
Execute this task:
- Skill path: <path-to-skill>
- Task: <eval prompt>
- Input files: <eval files if any, or "none">
- Save outputs to: <workspace>/iteration-<N>/eval-<ID>/with_skill/outputs/
- Outputs to save: <what the user cares about — e.g., "the .docx file", "the final CSV">
```

**Baseline run** (same prompt, but the baseline depends on context):
- **Creating a new skill**: no skill at all. Same prompt, no skill path, save to `without_skill/outputs/`.
- **Improving an existing skill**: the old version. Before editing, snapshot the skill (`cp -r <skill-path> <workspace>/skill-snapshot/`), then point the baseline subagent at the snapshot. Save to `old_skill/outputs/`.

Write an `eval_metadata.json` for each test case (assertions can be empty for now). Give each eval a descriptive name based on what it's testing — not just "eval-0". Use this name for the directory too. If this iteration uses new or modified eval prompts, create these files for each new eval directory — don't assume they carry over from previous iterations.

```json
{
  "eval_id": 0,
  "eval_name": "descriptive-name-here",
  "prompt": "The user's task prompt",
  "assertions": []
}
```

### Step 2: While runs are in progress, draft assertions

Don't just wait for the runs to finish — you can use this time productively. Draft quantitative assertions for each test case and explain them to the user. If assertions already exist in `evals/evals.json`, review them and explain what they check.

Good assertions are objectively verifiable and have descriptive names — they should read clearly in the benchmark viewer so someone glancing at the results immediately understands what each one checks. Subjective skills (writing style, design quality) are better evaluated qualitatively — don't force assertions onto things that need human judgment.

Update the `eval_metadata.json` files and `evals/evals.json` with the assertions once drafted. Also explain to the user what they'll see in the viewer — both the qualitative outputs and the quantitative benchmark.

### Step 3: As runs complete, capture timing data

When each subagent task completes, you receive a notification containing `total_tokens` and `duration_ms`. Save this data immediately to `timing.json` in the run directory:

```json
{
  "total_tokens": 84852,
  "duration_ms": 23332,
  "total_duration_seconds": 23.3
}
```

This is the only opportunity to capture this data — it comes through the task notification and isn't persisted elsewhere. Process each notification as it arrives rather than trying to batch them.

### Step 4: Grade, aggregate, and launch the viewer

Once all runs are done:

1. **Grade each run** — spawn a grader subagent (or grade inline) that reads `agents/grader.md` and evaluates each assertion against the outputs. Save results to `grading.json` in each run directory. The grading.json expectations array must use the fields `text`, `passed`, and `evidence` (not `name`/`met`/`details` or other variants) — the viewer depends on these exact field names. For assertions that can be checked programmatically, write and run a script rather than eyeballing it — scripts are faster, more reliable, and can be reused across iterations.

2. **Aggregate into benchmark** — run the aggregation script from the skill-creator directory:
   ```bash
   python -m scripts.aggregate_benchmark <workspace>/iteration-N --skill-name <name>
   ```
   This produces `benchmark.json` and `benchmark.md` with pass_rate, time, and tokens for each configuration, with mean ± stddev and the delta. If generating benchmark.json manually, see `references/schemas.md` for the exact schema the viewer expects.
Put each with_skill version before its baseline counterpart.

3. **Do an analyst pass** — read the benchmark data and surface patterns the aggregate stats might hide. See `agents/analyzer.md` (the "Analyzing Benchmark Results" section) for what to look for — things like assertions that always pass regardless of skill (non-discriminating), high-variance evals (possibly flaky), and time/token tradeoffs.

4. **Launch the viewer** with both qualitative outputs and quantitative data:
   ```bash
   nohup python <skill-creator-path>/eval-viewer/generate_review.py \
     <workspace>/iteration-N \
     --skill-name "my-skill" \
     --benchmark <workspace>/iteration-N/benchmark.json \
     > /dev/null 2>&1 &
   VIEWER_PID=$!
   ```
   For iteration 2+, also pass `--previous-workspace <workspace>/iteration-<N-1>`.

   **Cowork / headless environments:** If `webbrowser.open()` is not available or the environment has no display, use `--static <output_path>` to write a standalone HTML file instead of starting a server. Feedback will be downloaded as a `feedback.json` file when the user clicks "Submit All Reviews". After download, copy `feedback.json` into the workspace directory for the next iteration to pick up.

Note: please use generate_review.py to create the viewer; there's no need to write custom HTML.

5. **Tell the user** something like: "I've opened the results in your browser. There are two tabs — 'Outputs' lets you click through each test case and leave feedback, 'Benchmark' shows the quantitative comparison. When you're done, come back here and let me know."

### What the user sees in the viewer

The "Outputs" tab shows one test case at a time:
- **Prompt**: the task that was given
- **Output**: the files the skill produced, rendered inline where possible
- **Previous Output** (iteration 2+): collapsed section showing last iteration's output
- **Formal Grades** (if grading was run): collapsed section showing assertion pass/fail
- **Feedback**: a textbox that auto-saves as they type
- **Previous Feedback** (iteration 2+): their comments from last time, shown below the textbox

The "Benchmark" tab shows the stats summary: pass rates, timing, and token usage for each configuration, with per-eval breakdowns and analyst observations.

Navigation is via prev/next buttons or arrow keys. When done, they click "Submit All Reviews" which saves all feedback to `feedback.json`.

### Step 5: Read the feedback

When the user tells you they're done, read `feedback.json`:

```json
{
  "reviews": [
    {"run_id": "eval-0-with_skill", "feedback": "the chart is missing axis labels", "timestamp": "..."},
    {"run_id": "eval-1-with_skill", "feedback": "", "timestamp": "..."},
    {"run_id": "eval-2-with_skill", "feedback": "perfect, love this", "timestamp": "..."}
  ],
  "status": "complete"
}
```

Empty feedback means the user thought it was fine. Focus your improvements on the test cases where the user had specific complaints.

Kill the viewer server when you're done with it:

```bash
kill $VIEWER_PID 2>/dev/null
```

---

## Improving the skill

This is the heart of the loop. You've run the test cases, the user has reviewed the results, and now you need to make the skill better based on their feedback.

### How to think about improvements

1. **Generalize from the feedback.** The big picture thing that's happening here is that we're trying to create skills that can be used a million times (maybe literally, maybe even more who knows) across many different prompts. Here you and the user are iterating on only a few examples over and over again because it helps move faster. The user knows these examples in and out and it's quick for them to assess new outputs. But if the skill you and the user are codeveloping works only for those examples, it's useless. Rather than put in fiddly overfitty changes, or oppressively constrictive MUSTs, if there's some stubborn issue, you might try branching out and using different metaphors, or recommending different patterns of working. It's relatively cheap to try and maybe you'll land on something great.

2. **Keep the prompt lean.** Remove things that aren't pulling their weight. Make sure to read the transcripts, not just the final outputs — if it looks like the skill is making the model waste a bunch of time doing things that are unproductive, you can try getting rid of the parts of the skill that are making it do that and seeing what happens.

3. **Explain the why.** Try hard to explain the **why** behind everything you're asking the model to do. Today's LLMs are *smart*. They have good theory of mind and when given a good harness can go beyond rote instructions and really make things happen. Even if the feedback from the user is terse or frustrated, try to actually understand the task and why the user is writing what they wrote, and what they actually wrote, and then transmit this understanding into the instructions. If you find yourself writing ALWAYS or NEVER in all caps, or using super rigid structures, that's a yellow flag — if possible, reframe and explain the reasoning so that the model understands why the thing you're asking for is important. That's a more humane, powerful, and effective approach.

4. **Look for repeated work across test cases.** Read the transcripts from the test runs and notice if the subagents all independently wrote similar helper scripts or took the same multi-step approach to something. If all 3 test cases resulted in the subagent writing a `create_docx.py` or a `build_chart.py`, that's a strong signal the skill should bundle that script. Write it once, put it in `scripts/`, and tell the skill to use it. This saves every future invocation from reinventing the wheel.

This task is pretty important (we are trying to create billions a year in economic value here!) and your thinking time is not the blocker; take your time and really mull things over. I'd suggest writing a draft revision and then looking at it anew and making improvements. Really do your best to get into the head of the user and understand what they want and need.

### The iteration loop

After improving the skill:

1. Apply your improvements to the skill
2. Rerun all test cases into a new `iteration-<N+1>/` directory, including baseline runs. If you're creating a new skill, the baseline is always `without_skill` (no skill) — that stays the same across iterations. If you're improving an existing skill, use your judgment on what makes sense as the baseline: the original version the user came in with, or the previous iteration.
3. Launch the reviewer with `--previous-workspace` pointing at the previous iteration
4. Wait for the user to review and tell you they're done
5. Read the new feedback, improve again, repeat

Keep going until:
- The user says they're happy
- The feedback is all empty (everything looks good)
- You're not making meaningful progress

---

## Advanced: Blind comparison

For situations where you want a more rigorous comparison between two versions of a skill (e.g., the user asks "is the new version actually better?"), there's a blind comparison system. Read `agents/comparator.md` and `agents/analyzer.md` for the details. The basic idea is: give two outputs to an independent agent without telling it which is which, and let it judge quality. Then analyze why the winner won.

This is optional, requires subagents, and most users won't need it. The human review loop is usually sufficient.

---

## Description Optimization

The description field in SKILL.md frontmatter is the primary mechanism that determines whether Claude invokes a skill. After creating or improving a skill, offer to optimize the description for better triggering accuracy.

### Step 1: Generate trigger eval queries

Create 20 eval queries — a mix of should-trigger and should-not-trigger. Save as JSON:

```json
[
  {"query": "the user prompt", "should_trigger": true},
  {"query": "another prompt", "should_trigger": false}
]
```

The queries must be realistic and something a Claude Code or Claude.ai user would actually type. Not abstract requests, but requests that are concrete and specific and have a good amount of detail. For instance, file paths, personal context about the user's job or situation, column names and values, company names, URLs. A little bit of backstory. Some might be in lowercase or contain abbreviations or typos or casual speech. Use a mix of different lengths, and focus on edge cases rather than making them clear-cut (the user will get a chance to sign off on them).

Bad: `"Format this data"`, `"Extract text from PDF"`, `"Create a chart"`

Good: `"ok so my boss just sent me this xlsx file (its in my downloads, called something like 'Q4 sales final FINAL v2.xlsx') and she wants me to add a column that shows the profit margin as a percentage. The revenue is in column C and costs are in column D i think"`

For the **should-trigger** queries (8-10), think about coverage. You want different phrasings of the same intent — some formal, some casual. Include cases where the user doesn't explicitly name the skill or file type but clearly needs it. Throw in some uncommon use cases and cases where this skill competes with another but should win.

For the **should-not-trigger** queries (8-10), the most valuable ones are the near-misses — queries that share keywords or concepts with the skill but actually need something different. Think adjacent domains, ambiguous phrasing where a naive keyword match would trigger but shouldn't, and cases where the query touches on something the skill does but in a context where another tool is more appropriate.

The key thing to avoid: don't make should-not-trigger queries obviously irrelevant. "Write a fibonacci function" as a negative test for a PDF skill is too easy — it doesn't test anything. The negative cases should be genuinely tricky.

### Step 2: Review with user

Present the eval set to the user for review using the HTML template:

1. Read the template from `assets/eval_review.html`
2. Replace the placeholders:
   - `__EVAL_DATA_PLACEHOLDER__` → the JSON array of eval items (no quotes around it — it's a JS variable assignment)
   - `__SKILL_NAME_PLACEHOLDER__` → the skill's name
   - `__SKILL_DESCRIPTION_PLACEHOLDER__` → the skill's current description
3. Write to a temp file (e.g., `/tmp/eval_review_<skill-name>.html`) and open it: `open /tmp/eval_review_<skill-name>.html`
4. The user can edit queries, toggle should-trigger, add/remove entries, then click "Export Eval Set"
5. The file downloads to `~/Downloads/eval_set.json` — check the Downloads folder for the most recent version in case there are multiple (e.g., `eval_set (1).json`)

This step matters — bad eval queries lead to bad descriptions.

### Step 3: Run the optimization loop

Tell the user: "This will take some time — I'll run the optimization loop in the background and check on it periodically."

Save the eval set to the workspace, then run in the background:

```bash
python -m scripts.run_loop \
  --eval-set <path-to-trigger-eval.json> \
  --skill-path <path-to-skill> \
  --model <model-id-powering-this-session> \
  --max-iterations 5 \
  --verbose
```

Use the model ID from your system prompt (the one powering the current session) so the triggering test matches what the user actually experiences.

While it runs, periodically tail the output to give the user updates on which iteration it's on and what the scores look like.

This handles the full optimization loop automatically. It splits the eval set into 60% train and 40% held-out test, evaluates the current description (running each query 3 times to get a reliable trigger rate), then calls Claude to propose improvements based on what failed. It re-evaluates each new description on both train and test, iterating up to 5 times. When it's done, it opens an HTML report in the browser showing the results per iteration and returns JSON with `best_description` — selected by test score rather than train score to avoid overfitting.

### How skill triggering works

Understanding the triggering mechanism helps design better eval queries. Skills appear in Claude's `available_skills` list with their name + description, and Claude decides whether to consult a skill based on that description. The important thing to know is that Claude only consults skills for tasks it can't easily handle on its own — simple, one-step queries like "read this PDF" may not trigger a skill even if the description matches perfectly, because Claude can handle them directly with basic tools. Complex, multi-step, or specialized queries reliably trigger skills when the description matches.

This means your eval queries should be substantive enough that Claude would actually benefit from consulting a skill. Simple queries like "read file X" are poor test cases — they won't trigger skills regardless of description quality.

### Step 4: Apply the result

Take `best_description` from the JSON output and update the skill's SKILL.md frontmatter. Show the user before/after and report the scores.

---

### Package and Present (only if a file-delivery tool is available)

Check whether you have access to a tool that presents files to the user — `present_files`, or `SendUserFile` in Cowork remote. If you have neither, skip this step. If you do, package the skill and send the user the resulting `.skill` file with that tool:

```bash
python -m scripts.package_skill <path/to/skill-folder>
```

The presented `.skill` (or bare `SKILL.md`) file card shows a **Save skill** button when the user's org allows skill creation; clicking it installs the skill into their profile.

---

## Claude.ai-specific instructions

In Claude.ai, the core workflow is the same (draft → test → review → improve → repeat), but because Claude.ai doesn't have subagents, some mechanics change. Here's what to adapt:

**Running test cases**: No subagents means no parallel execution. For each test case, read the skill's SKILL.md, then follow its instructions to accomplish the test prompt yourself. Do them one at a time. This is less rigorous than independent subagents (you wrote the skill and you're also running it, so you have full context), but it's a useful sanity check — and the human review step compensates. Skip the baseline runs — just use the skill to complete the task as requested.

**Reviewing results**: If you can't open a browser (e.g., Claude.ai's VM has no display, or you're on a remote server), skip the browser reviewer entirely. Instead, present results directly in the conversation. For each test case, show the prompt and the output. If the output is a file the user needs to see (like a .docx or .xlsx), save it to the filesystem and tell them where it is so they can download and inspect it. Ask for feedback inline: "How does this look? Anything you'd change?"

**Benchmarking**: Skip the quantitative benchmarking — it relies on baseline comparisons which aren't meaningful without subagents. Focus on qualitative feedback from the user.

**The iteration loop**: Same as before — improve the skill, rerun the test cases, ask for feedback — just without the browser reviewer in the middle. You can still organize results into iteration directories on the filesystem if you have one.

**Description optimization**: This section requires the `claude` CLI tool (specifically `claude -p`) which is only available in Claude Code. Skip it if you're on Claude.ai.

**Blind comparison**: Requires subagents. Skip it.

**Packaging**: The `package_skill.py` script works anywhere with Python and a filesystem. On Claude.ai, you can run it and the user can download the resulting `.skill` file.

**Updating an existing skill**: The user might be asking you to update an existing skill, not create a new one. In this case:
- **Preserve the original name.** Note the skill's directory name and `name` frontmatter field -- use them unchanged. E.g., if the installed skill is `research-helper`, output `research-helper.skill` (not `research-helper-v2`).
- **Copy to a writeable location before editing.** The installed skill path may be read-only. Copy to `/tmp/skill-name/`, edit there, and package from the copy.
- **If packaging manually, stage in `/tmp/` first**, then copy to the output directory -- direct writes may fail due to permissions.

---

## Cowork-Specific Instructions

If you're in Cowork, the main things to know are:

- You have subagents, so the main workflow (spawn test cases in parallel, run baselines, grade, etc.) all works. (However, if you run into severe problems with timeouts, it's OK to run the test prompts in series rather than parallel.)
- You don't have a browser or display, so when generating the eval viewer, use `--static <output_path>` to write a standalone HTML file instead of starting a server. Then proffer a link that the user can click to open the HTML in their browser.
- For whatever reason, the Cowork setup seems to disincline Claude from generating the eval viewer after running the tests, so just to reiterate: whether you're in Cowork or in Claude Code, after running tests, you should always generate the eval viewer for the human to look at examples before revising the skill yourself and trying to make corrections, using `generate_review.py` (not writing your own boutique html code). Sorry in advance but I'm gonna go all caps here: GENERATE THE EVAL VIEWER *BEFORE* evaluating inputs yourself. You want to get them in front of the human ASAP!
- Feedback works differently: since there's no running server, the viewer's "Submit All Reviews" button will download `feedback.json` as a file. You can then read it from there (you may have to request access first).
- Packaging works — `package_skill.py` just needs Python and a filesystem.
- Description optimization (`run_loop.py` / `run_eval.py`) should work in Cowork just fine since it uses `claude -p` via subprocess, not a browser, but please save it until you've fully finished making the skill and the user agrees it's in good shape.
- **Updating an existing skill**: The user might be asking you to update an existing skill, not create a new one. Follow the update guidance in the claude.ai section above.

---

## Reference files

The agents/ directory contains instructions for specialized subagents. Read them when you need to spawn the relevant subagent.

- `agents/grader.md` — How to evaluate assertions against outputs
- `agents/comparator.md` — How to do blind A/B comparison between two outputs
- `agents/analyzer.md` — How to analyze why one version beat another

The references/ directory has additional documentation:
- `references/schemas.md` — JSON structures for evals.json, grading.json, etc.

---

Repeating one more time the core loop here for emphasis:

- Figure out what the skill is about
- Draft or edit the skill
- Run claude-with-access-to-the-skill on test prompts
- With the user, evaluate the outputs:
  - Create benchmark.json and run `eval-viewer/generate_review.py` to help the user review them
  - Run quantitative evals
- Repeat until you and the user are satisfied
- Package the final skill and return it to the user.

Please add steps to your TodoList, if you have such a thing, to make sure you don't forget. If you're in Cowork, please specifically put "Create evals JSON and run `eval-viewer/generate_review.py` so human can review test cases" in your TodoList to make sure it happens.

Good luck!

#!/bin/sh
basedir=$(dirname "$(echo "$0" | sed -e 's,\\,/,g')")

case `uname` in
    *CYGWIN*|*MINGW*|*MSYS*)
        if command -v cygpath > /dev/null 2>&1; then
            basedir=`cygpath -w "$basedir"`
        fi
    ;;
esac

if [ -x "$basedir/node" ]; then
  exec "$basedir/node"  "$basedir/node_modules/@googleworkspace/cli/run.js" "$@"
else 
  exec node  "$basedir/node_modules/@googleworkspace/cli/run.js" "$@"
fi

# gmail +send

> **PREREQUISITE:** Read `../gws-shared/SKILL.md` for auth, global flags, and security rules. If missing, run `gws generate-skills` to create it.

Send an email

## Usage

```bash
gws gmail +send --to <EMAILS> --subject <SUBJECT> --body <TEXT>
```

## Flags

| Flag | Required | Default | Description |
|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `--to` | ✓ | — | Recipient email address(es), comma-separated |
| `--subject` | ✓ | — | Email subject |
| `--body` | ✓ | — | Email body (plain text, or HTML with --html) |
| `--from` | — | — | Sender address (for send-as/alias; omit to use account default) |
| `--attach` | — | — | Attach a file (can be specified multiple times) |
| `--cc` | — | — | CC email address(es), comma-separated |
| `--bcc` | — | — | BCC email address(es), comma-separated |
| `--html` | — | — | Treat --body as HTML content (default is plain text) |
| `--dry-run` | — | — | Show the request that would be sent without executing it |
| `--draft` | — | — | Save as draft instead of sending |

## Examples

```bash
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Hello' --body 'Hi Alice!'
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Hello' --body 'Hi!' --cc bob@example.com
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Hello' --body '<b>Bold</b> text' --html
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Hello' --body 'Hi!' --from alias@example.com
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Report' --body 'See attached' -a report.pdf
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Files' --body 'Two files' -a a.pdf -a b.csv
gws gmail +send --to alice@example.com --subject 'Hello' --body 'Hi!' --draft
```

## Tips

- Handles RFC 5322 formatting, MIME encoding, and base64 automatically.
- Use --from to send from a configured send-as alias instead of your primary address.
- Use -a/--attach to add file attachments. Can be specified multiple times. Total size limit: 25MB.
- With --html, use fragment tags (<p>, <b>, <a>, <br>, etc.) — no <html>/<body> wrapper needed.
- Use --draft to save the message as a draft instead of sending it immediately.

# Skill parity baseline

Declared single-runtime skills. `check-skills.py` fails on **undeclared** asymmetry only:
when you deliberately add a skill to one runtime and not the other, add it here in the same
commit. When a skill shows up as undeclared drift, either activate it for both runtimes or
declare it here **with a reason**.

Every entry below carries its justification. An entry without one is not a decision, it is a
snapshot — which is what this file was until the audit noted below.

**Audited 2026-07-20.** The previous baseline (snapshotted 2026-07-19, claude=171 codex=147)
listed names with no reasons, so nobody could tell a deliberate split from accidental drift.
The audit classified every entry by provenance and by what the skill actually depends on.
Result: five skills were genuine gaps and were ported to Codex (`site-teardown`,
`pull-submissions`, `shadcn`, `tdd`, `lovable-prompt`). Current: **claude=175 codex=152**.

Note that DB parity is not activation. The DB is the complete catalog; Codex's activated set
in `.agents/skills/` is a deliberately small ops core. Porting a skill here does not activate
it, and is not meant to.

\"\").lower().endswith(\"@cal.com\"):\n            flagged.append({\"summary\": e.get(\"summary\"), \"start\": e[\"start\"].get(\"dateTime\"),\n                            \"iCalUID\": e.get(\"iCalUID\"), \"why\": \"title matches but not a Cal.com booking\"})\n            continue\n        found.append(to_consult(e))\n    return found, flagged\n\n\ndef get_event(event_id: str) -> dict | None:\n    \"\"\"None when the event is gone or cancelled.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        e = gws(\"calendar\", \"events\", \"get\", \"--params\",\n                json.dumps({\"calendarId\": CALENDAR_ID, \"eventId\": event_id}), \"--format\", \"json\")\n    except GwsError as ex:\n        if ex.code in (404, 410):\n            return None\n        raise\n    if e.get(\"status\") == \"cancelled\":\n        return None\n    return e\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Vault log (wiki/PRE-CONSULT-LOG.md): one row per consultation, keyed by\n# iCalUID, updated in place. The send ledger is append-only and separate.\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\nLOG_HEADER = \"\"\"---\ntitle: Pre-consultation check-in log\ntype: operating-layer\nrecord_kind: primary\ntags: [operating-layer, correspondence, consultations]\ncreated: 2026-09-11\nstatus: active\nsubject: Pre-consultation check-in log\naliases: [PRE-CONSULT-LOG]\n---\n\n# Pre-consultation check-in log\n\nOne row per Cal.com consultation the `pre-consultation-check-in` skill has\nhandled. Written by `scripts/checkin.py`; safe to read, do not hand-edit rows\n(the script rewrites the table). To stop a scheduled send, run\n`checkin.py cancel --event-id <id>` rather than deleting the row.\n\n**Status meanings**\n\n- `scheduled` — a one-shot Windows task exists and will send 30 minutes before the consultation\n- `sent` / `sent-late` — the email went out (late = fired after T-30 but still before the start, e.g. the laptop was asleep)\n- `sent-now` — T-30 had already passed when the skill ran, so it sent immediately\n- `cancelled` — the booking disappeared or was cancelled before the send; nothing sent\n- `moved` — the start time changed; the old task was replaced (a new `scheduled` row follows)\n- `missed` — the task could not run before the consultation started; nothing sent\n- `failed` — the send errored; see Note and logs/checkin.log\n- `cancelled-by-joshua` — Joshua opted this one out; the skill will not re-create it\n\nRelated: [[send-ledger]] gets one row when a send is scheduled and one when it goes out.\n\n## Log\n\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef load_log() -> dict[str, dict]:\n    if not LOG_MD.exists():\n        return {}\n    rows: dict[str, dict] = {}\n    for line in LOG_MD.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").splitlines():\n        if not line.startswith(\"|\") or line.startswith(\"| Consultation\") or line.startswith(\"|---\"):\n            continue\n        cells = [c.strip() for c in line.strip().strip(\"|\").split(\"|\")]\n        if len(cells) != len(COLUMNS) or not cells[-1]:\n            continue\n        rows[cells[-1]] = dict(zip(COLUMNS, cells))\n    return rows\n\n\ndef save_log(rows: dict[str, dict]) -> None:\n    LOG_MD.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    head = LOG_HEADER\n    if LOG_MD.exists():\n        text = LOG_MD.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        if \"## Log\" in text:\n            head = text.split(\"## Log\")[0] + \"## Log\\n\\n\"\n    lines = [\"| \" + \" | \".join(COLUMNS) + \" |\", \"|\" + \"---|\" * len(COLUMNS)]\n    for r in sorted(rows.values(), key=lambda r: r[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"]):\n        lines.append(\"| \" + \" | \".join(str(r.get(c, \"\")).replace(\"|\", \"/\") for c in COLUMNS) + \" |\")\n    LOG_MD.write_text(head + \"\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n\ndef upsert(rows: dict[str, dict], c: Consult, status: str, fires: str = \"\", task: str = \"\", note: str = \"\") -> None:\n    rows[c.ical_uid] = {\n        \"Consultation (Toronto)\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"Student\": c.name, \"Email\": c.email or \"\",\n        \"Status\": status, \"Fires at\": fires, \"Task\": task, \"Note\": note,\n        \"Event ID\": c.event_id, \"iCalUID\": c.ical_uid,\n    }\n\n\ndef ledger(recipient: str, what: str, status: str, note: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Append one row to wiki/send-ledger.md (append-only by rule).\"\"\"\n    if not LEDGER_MD.exists():\n        runlog(f\"ledger missing at {LEDGER_MD}; skipped row: {recipient} {what} {status}\")\n        return\n    row = f\"| {now():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} | {recipient} | {what} | {status} | {note} |\\n\"\n    with LEDGER_MD.open(\"a\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        f.write(row)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Windows Task Scheduler\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef powershell(script: str) -> str:\n    p = subprocess.run([\"powershell\", \"-NoProfile\", \"-NonInteractive\", \"-Command\", \"-\"],\n                       input=script, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    if p.returncode != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError((p.stderr or p.stdout)[-1500:])\n    return p.stdout\n\n\ndef pythonw() -> str:\n    exe = Path(sys.executable)\n    cand = exe.with_name(\"pythonw.exe\")\n    return str(cand if cand.exists() else exe)\n\n\ndef register_task(c: Consult, fire: datetime) -> str:\n    \"\"\"One-shot task at `fire` (local time). Runs as the logged-on user so gws\n    can reach the keyring. If the laptop is asleep at T-30 it wakes or catches\n    up when available; the task expires at the consultation start, because a\n    check-in after the meeting began is worse than none.\"\"\"\n    name = task_name(c.start_dt, c.name)\n    fire_local = fire.astimezone()          # machine-local for Task Scheduler\n    end_local = c.start_dt.astimezone()\n    args = f'\"{Path(__file__).resolve()}\" send --event-id {c.event_id}'\n    ps = f\"\"\"\n$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'\n$action  = New-ScheduledTaskAction -Execute '{pythonw()}' -Argument '{args}' -WorkingDirectory '{SKILL_DIR}'\n$trigger = New-ScheduledTaskTrigger -Once -At ([datetime]'{fire_local:%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S}')\n$trigger.EndBoundary = '{end_local:%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S}'\n$settings = New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet -StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun -AllowStartIfOnBatteries -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries -MultipleInstances IgnoreNew -ExecutionTimeLimit (New-TimeSpan -Minutes 10) -DeleteExpiredTaskAfter (New-TimeSpan -Hours 2)\n$principal = New-ScheduledTaskPrincipal -UserId \"$env:USERDOMAIN\\\\$env:USERNAME\" -LogonType Interactive -RunLevel Limited\nRegister-ScheduledTask -TaskName '{name}' -TaskPath '{TASK_PATH}' -Action $action -Trigger $trigger -Settings $settings -Principal $principal -Description 'LSAT U pre-consultation check-in for {c.name} ({fmt(c.start_dt)}). Created by pre-consultation-check-in skill.' -Force | Out-Null\n(Get-ScheduledTask -TaskName '{name}' -TaskPath '{TASK_PATH}').State\n\"\"\"\n    state = powershell(ps).strip()\n    runlog(f\"registered task {name} fire={fmt(fire)} state={state}\")\n    return name\n\n\ndef unregister_task(name: str) -> bool:\n    if not name:\n        return False\n    try:\n        powershell(f\"Unregister-ScheduledTask -TaskName '{name}' -TaskPath '{TASK_PATH}' -Confirm:$false -ErrorAction Stop\")\n        runlog(f\"unregistered task {name}\")\n        return True\n    except RuntimeError as ex:\n        runlog(f\"unregister {name} failed: {ex}\")\n        return False\n\n\ndef list_tasks() -> list[dict]:\n    ps = f\"\"\"\n$ErrorActionPreference = 'SilentlyContinue'\nGet-ScheduledTask -TaskPath '{TASK_PATH}' | ForEach-Object {{\n  $i = $_ | Get-ScheduledTaskInfo\n  [pscustomobject]@{{ name=$_.TaskName; state=[string]$_.State; next=[string]$i.NextRunTime; last=[string]$i.LastRunTime; result=$i.LastTaskResult }}\n}} | ConvertTo-Json -Compress\n\"\"\"\n    out = powershell(ps).strip()\n    if not out:\n        return []\n    data = json.loads(out)\n    return data if isinstance(data, list) else [data]\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# The send itself (what the scheduled task runs)\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef compose(c: Consult) -> tuple[str, str]:\n    return SUBJECT, BODY.format(first=first_name(c.name))\n\n\ndef send_email(c: Consult, dry_run: bool) -> dict:\n    subject, body = compose(c)\n    args = [\"gmail\", \"+send\", \"--to\", c.email, \"--subject\", subject, \"--body\", body, \"--format\", \"json\"]\n    if dry_run:\n        args.append(\"--dry-run\")\n    return gws(*args)\n\n\ndef do_send(event_id: str, dry_run: bool = False, force: bool = False, mode: str = \"task\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Verify, then send. Returns a result dict; never raises on a\n    'do not send' outcome (those are logged as statuses).\"\"\"\n    rows = load_log()\n    e = get_event(event_id)\n    if e is None:\n        c = next((r for r in rows.values() if r[\"Event ID\"] == event_id), None)\n        if c and not dry_run:\n            c_obj = Consult(event_id, c[\"iCalUID\"], c[\"Student\"], c[\"Email\"], c[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"].replace(\" \", \"T\") + \":00\" + now().strftime(\"%z\")[:3] + \":\" + now().strftime(\"%z\")[3:], \"\", None)\n            upsert(rows, c_obj, \"cancelled\", note=f\"booking gone at {fmt(now())}; nothing sent\")\n            unregister_task(c[\"Task\"])\n            save_log(rows)\n        runlog(f\"send {event_id}: event gone/cancelled; nothing sent\")\n        return {\"status\": \"cancelled\", \"event_id\": event_id}\n\n    if not CONSULT_RE.match(e.get(\"summary\", \"\")):\n        runlog(f\"send {event_id}: title no longer a consultation: {e.get('summary')!r}\")\n        return {\"status\": \"not-a-consultation\", \"summary\": e.get(\"summary\")}\n\n    c = to_consult(e)\n    prev = rows.get(c.ical_uid, {})\n    t = now()\n\n    if not c.email:\n        upsert(rows, c, \"failed\", note=\"no student attendee on the event\")\n        save_log(rows)\n        return {\"status\": \"failed\", \"reason\": \"no attendee email\"}\n\n    if prev.get(\"Status\") in {\"sent\", \"sent-late\", \"sent-now\"} and not force:\n        runlog(f\"send {event_id}: already {prev['Status']}; refusing to double-send\")\n        return {\"status\": \"already-sent\", \"student\": c.name}\n\n    # Rescheduled since we registered? Compare to the logged start.\n    logged_start = prev.get(\"Consultation (Toronto)\")\n    if logged_start and logged_start != fmt(c.start_dt):\n        new_fire = c.fire_dt\n        if new_fire > t + timedelta(minutes=1):\n            unregister_task(prev.get(\"Task\", \"\"))\n            name = register_task(c, new_fire)\n            upsert(rows, c, \"scheduled\", fires=fmt(new_fire), task=name, note=f\"moved from {logged_start}; re-scheduled\")\n            save_log(rows)\n            runlog(f\"send {event_id}: moved {logged_start} -> {fmt(c.start_dt)}; re-registered {name}\")\n            return {\"status\": \"moved\", \"student\": c.name, \"new_start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"fires\": fmt(new_fire)}\n        # otherwise the new time is within 30 min (send now) or past (missed) - fall through\n\n    if t >= c.start_dt:\n        if not dry_run:\n            upsert(rows, c, \"missed\", note=f\"task ran at {fmt(t)}, after the start; nothing sent\")\n            unregister_task(prev.get(\"Task\", \"\"))\n            save_log(rows)\n        runlog(f\"send {event_id}: too late ({fmt(t)} >= {fmt(c.start_dt)}); nothing sent\")\n        return {\"status\": \"missed\", \"student\": c.name}\n\n    late = t > c.fire_dt + timedelta(minutes=5)\n    status = \"sent-now\" if mode == \"now\" else (\"sent-late\" if late else \"sent\")\n    subject, body = compose(c)\n    if dry_run:\n        out = SKILL_DIR / \"logs\" / f\"dryrun-{c.event_id}.txt\"\n        out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        out.write_text(f\"To: {c.email}\\nSubject: {subject}\\nWould-be status: {status}\\nAt: {fmt(t)}\\n\\n{body}\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        runlog(f\"send {event_id}: DRY RUN ok -> {out.name}\")\n        return {\"status\": \"dry-run\", \"student\": c.name, \"to\": c.email, \"subject\": subject, \"body\": body, \"file\": str(out)}\n\n    try:\n        resp = send_email(c, dry_run=False)\n    except Exception as ex:  # noqa: BLE001 - we want every failure in the log\n        upsert(rows, c, \"failed\", task=prev.get(\"Task\", \"\"), note=f\"{type(ex).__name__}: {str(ex)[:160]}\")\n        save_log(rows)\n        ledger(slug(c.name), f\"pre-consult check-in (consult {fmt(c.start_dt)})\", \"failed\", str(ex)[:120])\n        runlog(f\"send {event_id}: FAILED {ex}\")\n        return {\"status\": \"failed\", \"student\": c.name, \"error\": str(ex)}\n\n    msg_id = resp.get(\"id\") or resp.get(\"messageId\") or resp.get(\"_raw\", \"\")[:40]\n    upsert(rows, c, status, fires=prev.get(\"Fires at\", \"\"), task=\"\", note=f\"sent {fmt(t)} to {c.email}; msg {msg_id}\")\n    save_log(rows)\n    ledger(slug(c.name), f\"pre-consult check-in (consult {fmt(c.start_dt)})\", f\"sent {t:%Y-%m-%d}\",\n           f\"auto-send at T-30 via pre-consultation-check-in; Msg {msg_id}\")\n    unregister_task(prev.get(\"Task\", \"\"))\n    runlog(f\"send {event_id}: SENT to {c.email} msg={msg_id} status={status}\")\n    return {\"status\": status, \"student\": c.name, \"to\": c.email, \"message_id\": msg_id}\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Commands\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef window(args) -> tuple[datetime, datetime]:\n    t0 = now()\n    if getattr(args, \"until\", None):\n        d = datetime.strptime(args.until, \"%Y-%m-%d\").replace(tzinfo=TZ)\n        return t0, d + timedelta(days=1)\n    return t0, t0 + timedelta(days=args.days)\n\n\ndef coverage(c: Consult, rows: dict[str, dict]) -> str:\n    r = rows.get(c.ical_uid)\n    if not r:\n        return \"new\"\n    if r[\"Status\"] in COVERED and r[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"] == fmt(c.start_dt):\n        return f\"covered ({r['Status']})\"\n    if r[\"Status\"] in COVERED:\n        return f\"moved (was {r['Consultation (Toronto)']})\"\n    if r[\"Status\"] in OPTED_OUT:\n        return \"opted out\"\n    return f\"retry ({r['Status']})\"\n\n\ndef cmd_find(args) -> int:\n    t0, t1 = window(args)\n    found, flagged = list_consultations(t0, t1)\n    rows = load_log()\n    items = []\n    for c in found:\n        cov = coverage(c, rows)\n        fire = c.fire_dt\n        timing = \"send now\" if fire <= t0 < c.start_dt else (\"past\" if t0 >= c.start_dt else f\"fires {fmt(fire)}\")\n        items.append({**asdict(c), \"start_local\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"coverage\": cov, \"timing\": timing})\n    result = {\"window\": [fmt(t0), fmt(t1)], \"consultations\": items, \"flagged\": flagged}\n    if args.json:\n        print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))\n    else:\n        print(f\"Window {fmt(t0)} -> {fmt(t1)} (America/Toronto). {len(items)} consultation(s).\")\n        for i in items:\n            print(f\"  {i['start_local']}  {i['name']:<28} {i['email'] or '(no email)':<34} {i['coverage']:<22} {i['timing']}\")\n        for f in flagged:\n            print(f\"  FLAG {f['start']} {f['summary']!r}: {f['why']}\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef cmd_schedule(args) -> int:\n    t0, t1 = window(args)\n    found, flagged = list_consultations(t0, t1)\n    rows = load_log()\n    only = {s.lower() for s in (args.only or [])}\n    report = []\n    for c in found:\n        if only and not any(s in (c.name.lower(), c.ical_uid.lower(), c.event_id.lower(), (c.email or \"\").lower()) for s in only):\n            continue\n        cov = coverage(c, rows)\n        prev = rows.get(c.ical_uid, {})\n        what = f\"pre-consult check-in (consult {fmt(c.start_dt)})\"\n        if cov.startswith(\"covered\"):\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"skip\", \"why\": cov}); continue\n        if cov == \"opted out\" and not args.force:\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"skip\", \"why\": \"cancelled-by-joshua\"}); continue\n        if not c.email:\n            upsert(rows, c, \"failed\", note=\"no student attendee on the event\")\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"failed\", \"why\": \"no attendee email\"}); continue\n        if cov.startswith(\"moved\"):\n            unregister_task(prev.get(\"Task\", \"\"))\n        if t0 >= c.start_dt:\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"skip\", \"why\": \"already started\"}); continue\n        if c.fire_dt <= t0:\n            if args.dry_run:\n                report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"would send now\", \"to\": c.email}); continue\n            save_log(rows)\n            res = do_send(c.event_id, mode=\"now\")\n            rows = load_log()\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"sent now\", **{k: v for k, v in res.items() if k != \"student\"}})\n            continue\n        if args.dry_run:\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"would schedule\", \"fires\": fmt(c.fire_dt), \"to\": c.email}); continue\n        try:\n            name = register_task(c, c.fire_dt)\n        except Exception as ex:  # noqa: BLE001\n            upsert(rows, c, \"failed\", note=f\"task registration failed: {str(ex)[:160]}\")\n            report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"failed\", \"why\": str(ex)[:200]}); continue\n        note = f\"moved from {prev['Consultation (Toronto)']}\" if cov.startswith(\"moved\") else \"\"\n        upsert(rows, c, \"scheduled\", fires=fmt(c.fire_dt), task=name, note=note)\n        ledger(slug(c.name), what, \"scheduled\", f\"fires {fmt(c.fire_dt)} via Task Scheduler {TASK_PATH.rstrip(chr(92))}\\\\{name}; to {c.email}\")\n        report.append({\"student\": c.name, \"start\": fmt(c.start_dt), \"action\": \"scheduled\", \"fires\": fmt(c.fire_dt), \"to\": c.email, \"task\": name})\n    if not args.dry_run:\n        save_log(rows)\n    # Reconcile: anything we scheduled inside this window that vanished from the calendar was cancelled.\n    seen = {c.ical_uid for c in found}\n    for uid, r in list(rows.items()):\n        if r[\"Status\"] != \"scheduled\" or uid in seen:\n            continue\n        try:\n            st = datetime.strptime(r[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"], \"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\").replace(tzinfo=TZ)\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if t0 <= st <= t1 and not args.dry_run and get_event(r[\"Event ID\"]) is None:\n            unregister_task(r[\"Task\"])\n            r[\"Status\"], r[\"Task\"], r[\"Note\"] = \"cancelled\", \"\", f\"booking gone by {fmt(t0)}; task removed\"\n            report.append({\"student\": r[\"Student\"], \"start\": r[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"], \"action\": \"cancelled\", \"why\": \"booking no longer on the calendar\"})\n    if not args.dry_run:\n        save_log(rows)\n    result = {\"window\": [fmt(t0), fmt(t1)], \"dry_run\": args.dry_run, \"actions\": report, \"flagged\": flagged}\n    if args.json:\n        print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))\n    else:\n        print(f\"Window {fmt(t0)} -> {fmt(t1)}{' (dry run)' if args.dry_run else ''}. {len(report)} action(s).\")\n        for a in report:\n            extra = {k: v for k, v in a.items() if k not in ('student', 'start', 'action')}\n            print(f\"  {a['start']}  {a['student']:<28} {a['action']:<16} {json.dumps(extra) if extra else ''}\")\n        for f in flagged:\n            print(f\"  FLAG {f['start']} {f['summary']!r}: {f['why']}\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef cmd_send(args) -> int:\n    res = do_send(args.event_id, dry_run=args.dry_run, force=args.force)\n    print(json.dumps(res, indent=2))\n    return 0 if res.get(\"status\") not in {\"failed\"} else 1\n\n\ndef cmd_status(args) -> int:\n    rows = load_log()\n    tasks = {t[\"name\"]: t for t in list_tasks()}\n    t0 = now()\n    out = []\n    for r in sorted(rows.values(), key=lambda r: r[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"]):\n        try:\n            st = datetime.strptime(r[\"Consultation (Toronto)\"], \"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\").replace(tzinfo=TZ)\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if st < t0 - timedelta(days=args.past) :\n            continue\n        t = tasks.get(r[\"Task\"]) if r[\"Task\"] else None\n        out.append({**r, \"task_state\": (t or {}).get(\"state\", \"absent\" if r[\"Status\"] == \"scheduled\" else \"\"),\n                    \"task_next\": (t or {}).get(\"next\", \"\")})\n    orphans = [t for n, t in tasks.items() if not any(r[\"Task\"] == n for r in rows.values())]\n    if args.json:\n        print(json.dumps({\"rows\": out, \"orphan_tasks\": orphans}, indent=2))\n    else:\n        for r in out:\n            print(f\"  {r['Consultation (Toronto)']}  {r['Student']:<28} {r['Status']:<14} fires {r['Fires at'] or '-':<17} task:{r['task_state'] or '-':<8} {r['Note']}\")\n        for t in orphans:\n            print(f\"  ORPHAN task {t['name']} state={t['state']} (no log row)\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef cmd_cancel(args) -> int:\n    rows = load_log()\n    r = next((r for r in rows.values() if r[\"Event ID\"] == args.event_id or r[\"iCalUID\"] == args.event_id), None)\n    if not r:\n        print(json.dumps({\"status\": \"not-found\", \"event_id\": args.event_id})); return 1\n    unregister_task(r[\"Task\"])\n    r[\"Status\"], r[\"Task\"], r[\"Note\"] = \"cancelled-by-joshua\", \"\", f\"cancelled {fmt(now())}: {args.reason or 'no reason given'}\"\n    save_log(rows)\n    ledger(slug(r[\"Student\"]), f\"pre-consult check-in (consult {r['Consultation (Toronto)']})\", \"cancelled\", args.reason or \"Joshua cancelled the scheduled send\")\n    print(json.dumps({\"status\": \"cancelled-by-joshua\", \"student\": r[\"Student\"]}, indent=2))\n    return 0\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)\n    sub = p.add_subparsers(dest=\"cmd\", required=True)\n\n    def add_window(sp):\n        sp.add_argument(\"--days\", type=float, default=5, help=\"window length from now (default 5)\")\n        sp.add_argument(\"--until\", help=\"instead of --days: through the end of this date, YYYY-MM-DD (Toronto)\")\n        sp.add_argument(\"--json\", action=\"store_true\")\n\n    sp = sub.add_parser(\"find\", help=\"list consultations and their coverage\"); add_window(sp); sp.set_defaults(fn=cmd_find)\n    sp = sub.add_parser(\"schedule\", help=\"register T-30 tasks\"); add_window(sp)\n    sp.add_argument(\"--dry-run\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"report what would happen, change nothing\")\n    sp.add_argument(\"--only\", nargs=\"*\", help=\"restrict to these names / emails / ids\")\n    sp.add_argument(\"--force\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"re-create even if Joshua cancelled it before\")\n    sp.set_defaults(fn=cmd_schedule)\n    sp = sub.add_parser(\"send\", help=\"verify and send one check-in (what the task runs)\")\n    sp.add_argument(\"--event-id\", required=True); sp.add_argument(\"--dry-run\", action=\"store_true\")\n    sp.add_argument(\"--force\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"send even if the log says it already went\")\n    sp.set_defaults(fn=cmd_send)\n    sp = sub.add_parser(\"status\", help=\"scheduled / sent / failed, with Task Scheduler state\")\n    sp.add_argument(\"--past\", type=int, {"command": "cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os; P=\".agents/skills/pre-consultation-check-in/scripts/checkin.py\"; python - \"$P\" <<'EOF'\nimport sys\np=sys.argv[1]; s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()\nold='''def powershell(script: str) -> str:\n    p = subprocess.run([\"powershell\", \"-NoProfile\", \"-NonInteractive\", \"-Command\", \"-\"],\n                       input=script, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    if p.returncode != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError((p.stderr or p.stdout)[-1500:])\n    return p.stdout'''\nnew='''def powershell(script: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"-EncodedCommand: multi-line scripts via stdin silently produce nothing\n    on Windows PowerShell 5, so the script goes in as base64 UTF-16LE.\"\"\"\n    import base64\n    enc = base64.b64encode(script.encode(\"utf-16-le\")).decode(\"ascii\")\n    p = subprocess.run([\"powershell\", \"-NoProfile\", \"-NonInteractive\", \"-EncodedCommand\", enc],\n                       capture_output=True, text=True, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    if p.returncode != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError((p.stderr or p.stdout)[-1500:])\n    return p.stdout'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s); print(\"patched\")\nEOF\nexport PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8; python -W error -m py_compile \"$P\" && echo COMPILE_OK; python \"$P\" status; date", "description": "Switch PowerShell invocation to -EncodedCommand and re-run status"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\.agents\\skills\\pre-consultation-check-in\\references\\identification.md", "content": "# How a consultation is identified, and why\n\nEvidence base: 241 events on Joshua's primary Google Calendar, 2026-05-14 through\n2026-10-11 (pulled 2026-09-11). 70 were consultations. 171 were everything else:\npaid `<Name> session` bookings from LSAT U, `LSAT Session between Joshua and …`\nbookings from Cal.com, `Reduced Rate between …`, `Complimentary session between …`,\nbare-name manual sessions, church, webinars, \"booked off\".\n\n## The rule\n\n```\n^Consultation\\s+between\\s+Joshua\\s+and\\s+(.+)$\n```\n\napplied to the event title (`summary`).\n\n| Signal | Matches | False positives |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Title starts with `Consultation`** | 70 | **0** |\n| iCalUID ends `@Cal.com` | 74 | 4 (`Complimentary session between Joshua and …`, a free session, not a consult) |\n| Description contains `cal.com/booking` | 93 | 23 (the 4 complimentary + 19 paid `LSAT Session` / `Reduced Rate` bookings that came through Cal.com but were stamped `@google.com`) |\n| Description blurb `LSAT strategy call: to assess your baseline…` | 70 | 0, but it is the Cal.com event-type description; rewording it in Cal.com would silently drop every match |\n\nThe title is the only signal that is both perfect on the data and visible to Joshua\nevery day (so a change would be noticed). The original skill required all three of\nthe first rows together; the extra two add nothing and would mislead anyone who\nloosened the rule to \"any Cal.com booking\".\n\n## What every matched event looks like\n\n- Title spelled `Consultation  between Joshua and <Name>` with **two spaces** after\n  \"Consultation\", all 70 times. Cal.com builds it from the event-type name, which\n  carries a trailing space. `\\s+` in the regex survives a future fix.\n- `<Name>` is whatever the prospect typed on the Cal.com form: 50 full names, 20\n  first-name-only, several with a trailing space (`'Zeina '`, `'Sandra Kooh '`).\n  Strip it. First name = first token.\n- Exactly one attendee without `\"self\": true`. That is the student. Their email is\n  the only reliable address; the description repeats it but do not parse it from there.\n- `iCalUID` is `<bookingUid>@Cal.com`. The same `bookingUid` is in the reschedule\n  link (`https://cal.com/booking/<bookingUid>?changes=true`). It is the stable key\n  for the run log.\n- `start.timeZone` is always `America/Toronto`; duration always 30 minutes.\n- `hangoutLink` / `location` carry the Google Meet URL.\n- `status` is `confirmed`. A cancelled Cal.com booking does **not** appear in\n  `events list` at all unless `showDeleted` is set; `events get` on its id returns\n  `status: cancelled`. This is why the send step re-fetches by id rather than\n  trusting the earlier list.\n\n## The sanity check\n\nIf the title matches but the `iCalUID` is not `@Cal.com`, the event was made by\nhand (never happened in 241 events, but a test event or a manual booking would\nlook like this). `find` and `schedule` list it as **flagged** instead of silently\nincluding or dropping it; `schedule --include-flagged` schedules it anyway.\n\n## Near misses worth knowing\n\n- `Complimentary session between Joshua and Emily Khym` (Cal.com, free follow-up\n  session): not a consultation. Sending \"before our consultation today\" to\n  someone who already had the consultation reads as a mistake.\n- `LSAT Session   between Joshua and Lydia Lee` (Cal.com paid session, three\n  spaces): not a consultation.\n- `Joshua Fisseha - 30 minutes meeting` (another scheduler, no `@` in the UID):\n  not a consultation.\n- `Adriana — Transcript Intake/Review`: not a consultation.\n"}

Do not \"improve\" it: it is deliberately short, and\nthe reply it invites (\"still good\" / \"need to reschedule\") is the whole point.\n\n```\nFrom: Joshua <contact@aspiringattorneys.com>\nTo: <student address from the calendar event>\nSubject: Consultation check-in\n\nHi {First},\n\nJust checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good for our meeting.\n\nIf anything has come up or you need to reschedule, just let me know.\n\nBest,\n\nJoshua\n```\n\n## Facts about how it has always gone out\n\n- **Subject is `Consultation check-in`**, lowercase c, lowercase i. The older\n  spec file wrote \"Check-In\"; the mailbox says otherwise.\n- **Always a new email, never a reply.** None of the 201 carried `In-Reply-To`.\n  The post-consultation follow-up then replies *into* this thread\n  (`Re: Consultation check-in`), so this message is the root of the student's\n  thread. Do not go hunting for an earlier thread to reply in; for a first-time\n  prospect there usually is none (Cal.com sends the booking confirmation itself).\n- **Sent 30 minutes before the start**, to the second, from 2026-08-26 through\n  2026-09-03 (an earlier automation). Before that, by hand, 10-25 minutes out.\n- **\"today\"** is always true at T-30 for a 30-minute lead; the sentence needs no\n  date logic. If the lead time ever grows past a few hours, revisit this word.\n- **First name only**, taken from the Cal.com title (`Consultation between Joshua\n  and <Name>`), first token, trailing spaces stripped, capitalised only if the\n  prospect typed it all-lowercase. \"Hi Farid,\" and \"Hi Sandra,\" are both right.\n- Plain text. No HTML, no signature block, no Meet link (Joshua chose the fixed\n  body on 2026-09-11; the Cal.com confirmation already has the link).\n- Voice check against `wiki/teaching-voice.md`: \"Hi [Name],\" greeting, \"Best,\"\n  sign-off, signed \"Joshua\", \"just wanted to make sure\" and \"let me know\" are\n  his own signature phrases. No em dashes. Nothing to fix.\n\n## If Joshua changes the wording\n\nEdit `SUBJECT` / `BODY` at the top of `scripts/checkin.py` and this file\ntogether. Keep the subject stable unless he says otherwise: the follow-up skill\nand Gmail search both key on it.\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\.agents\\skills\\pre-consultation-check-in\\SKILL.md", "content": "---\nname: pre-consultation-check-in\ndescription: >-\n  Schedule and send Joshua's \"Consultation check-in\" email 30 minutes before each\n  upcoming Cal.com consultation, from his Google Calendar, with a run log so nothing\n  is sent twice. Use this whenever Joshua mentions pre-consult emails, consultation\n  check-ins or reminders, \"who has a consultation coming up\", \"schedule the check-ins\n  for the next N days\", \"did X get their check-in\", \"cancel the check-in for X\", or\n  asks what consultations are on the calendar this week. Also use it when he asks to\n  look at the calendar for consultations even if he does not say \"email\": this skill\n  is how consultations are found and how their coverage is reported. Not for paid\n  tutoring sessions (\"Sofia session\"), not for post-consultation follow-ups (that is\n  consultation-followup-drafts), not for booking links.\n---\n\n# Pre-consultation check-in\n\nThirty minutes before a consultation, the prospect gets a short email from Joshua:\n\"Just checking in before our consultation today…\". It has gone out 201 times with\nthat exact wording. This skill finds the consultations, registers one Windows\nscheduled task per consultation at T-30, and that task (a plain Python script, no\nLLM) re-verifies the booking and sends. Joshua granted standing permission on\n2026-09-11 for this email to go out without a per-send approval, so the skill's job\nis to be exact and to leave a clear record, not to ask.\n\nEverything runs through one script:\n\n```\npython \"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\.agents\\skills\\pre-consultation-check-in\\scripts\\checkin.py\" <command>\n```\n\n| Command | What it does |\n|---|---|\n| `find --days 5` | List consultations in the window and whether each is already covered |\n| `schedule --days 5` | Register the T-30 task for every uncovered one (sends immediately if T-30 already passed) |\n| `status` | Every row in the log with its Task Scheduler state |\n| `cancel --event-id <id> --reason \"...\"` | Remove a scheduled send and mark it so it is never re-created |\n| `send --event-id <id> [--dry-run]` | What the task runs. Use `--dry-run` to see the exact email for one event |\n\nAdd `--json` to `find`, `schedule`, `status` when you want to read the result\nprogrammatically. Use `--dry-run` on `schedule` when Joshua asks what *would* happen.\n\n## What counts as a consultation\n\nTitle matches `^Consultation\\s+between\\s+Joshua\\s+and\\s+(.+)$`. That is the whole\nrule. Verified 2026-09-11 against 241 calendar events: 70 of 70 consultations match,\n0 of 171 other events match. `<Name> session`, `LSAT Session between…`,\n`Complimentary session between…`, `Reduced Rate between…` are all something else,\neven though most of them also came through Cal.com. The script applies the rule; you\nnever re-derive it. Read `references/identification.md` before proposing any change\nto it, because the two \"extra\" signals in the old version of this skill (Cal.com link\nin the description, `@Cal.com` UID) each produce false positives on real data.\n\nA title match with a non-Cal.com UID is **flagged**, not scheduled. It has never\nhappened on a real booking. If Joshua made a consultation by hand and wants it\ncovered, run `schedule --include-flagged`.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **Work out the window.** Default is 5 days from now. Map his phrasing:\n   \"today\" → `--until <today YYYY-MM-DD>`; \"tomorrow\" → `--until <tomorrow>`;\n   \"next three days\" → `--days 3`; \"this week\" / \"through Friday\" → `--until <that\n   date>`. Dates are America/Toronto; the script handles the conversion.\n2. **Run `find`.** Show him the list as-is: start time, name, email, coverage\n   (`new`, `covered (scheduled)`, `covered (sent)`, `moved`, `opted out`), and\n   timing (`fires 2026-09-14 13:30`, `send now`, `past`). If he only asked who is\n   coming up or who is already covered, stop here.\n3. **Run `schedule`** with the same window. Do not ask for confirmation first; the\n   permission is standing. The one exception: flagged events. Tell him about those\n   and only add `--include-flagged` if he says so.\n4. **Report** the actions line by line (scheduled / sent now / skipped, with the\n   fire time and address) and point at `wiki/PRE-CONSULT-LOG.md`. If anything says\n   `failed`, quote the `why` and read `logs/checkin.log`.\n\n`schedule` is safe to run repeatedly. It reads the log first and skips anything\nalready `scheduled` or `sent` for the same booking (`iCalUID`) at the same start\ntime. Running it every morning for the next 5 days is the intended pattern.\n\n## What happens at T-30\n\nThe task runs `send --event-id <id>` under Joshua's login (so `gws` can reach the\nkeyring). Before sending it re-fetches the event, because a booking can change\nbetween scheduling and firing:\n\n- Booking cancelled or gone → logs `cancelled`, sends nothing, deletes its task.\n- Start time moved to later → registers a new task at the new T-30, logs `moved`.\n- Start time moved to within 30 minutes → sends now.\n- Consultation already started → logs `missed`, sends nothing. A check-in that\n  arrives after the meeting began is worse than none.\n- Already `sent` in the log → refuses to double-send.\n- Otherwise sends via `gws gmail +send` as a **new email** (never a reply; see\n  `references/email.md` for why), logs `sent` (or `sent-late` if the laptop was\n  asleep and the task caught up), appends to `wiki/send-ledger.md`, and deletes\n  its own task.\n\nThe task is set to wake the laptop, to run as soon as possible if it missed its\ntime, and to expire at the consultation start. It is a one-shot; this skill never\ncreates a recurring task.\n\n## Reading and changing state\n\n- `wiki/PRE-CONSULT-LOG.md`: one row per booking, rewritten by the script. Status\n  meanings are at the top of that file. name: ai-os-safety-rules
description: Delete stays blocked at the tool layer; email sends happen only when Joshua says "send" in chat (rule changed 2026-09-11), never on Claude's initiative.
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

Deleting (Gmail trash/delete, Drive delete) stays denied in the tool layer and in `CLAUDE.md`. Sending changed on 2026-09-11: draft by default, and when Joshua says "send it" in chat, send it and log it in `wiki/send-ledger.md`. The first send under the new rule was the Sandra booking reply (`gws gmail users drafts send` works; the deny list does not block it).

**Why:** He wanted "send" to mean send, without a second confirmation round. The old "draft, never send" line made me hedge after he had already said the word.
**How to apply:** A request to draft is never a request to send. Sending needs the word from him, in chat, for that specific email. Deleting still needs the deny list narrowed, never removed. Related: [[ai-os-vault-location]], [[email-apology-shape]]

"""Phase 3: generate brain/skills/INDEX.md — one line per DB skill,
grouped by source-type, nested within the claude/ and codex/ split."""
import os, re, sys

BRAIN = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", ".."))
DB = f"{BRAIN}/skills"

def read_desc(skill_dir):
    sk = os.path.join(skill_dir, "SKILL.md")
    if not os.path.isfile(sk):
        # resolve symlink dir
        return "", "no SKILL.md"
    try:
        with open(sk, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
            txt = f.read()
    except OSError:
        return "", "unreadable"
    m = re.search(r"(?ms)^---\s*\n(.*?)\n---", txt)
    fm = m.group(1) if m else txt[:800]
    dm = re.search(r"(?mi)^description:\s*(.*)$", fm)
    if not dm:
        return "", "no description"
    d = dm.group(1).strip()
    if d in (">", "|", ">-", "|-", ""):
        # folded / literal block scalar: gather the indented lines that follow
        rest = fm[dm.end():]
        block = []
        for line in rest.splitlines():
            if line.startswith((" ", "	")):

Tool result:
# JSON Schemas

This document defines the JSON schemas used by skill-creator.

---

## evals.json

Defines the evals for a skill. Located at `evals/evals.json` within the skill directory.

```json
{
  "skill_name": "example-skill",
  "evals": [
    {
      "id": 1,
      "prompt": "User's example prompt",
      "expected_output": "Description of expected result",
      "files": ["evals/files/sample1.pdf"],
      "expectations": [
        "The output includes X",
        "The skill used script Y"
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

**Fields:**
- `skill_name`: Name matching the skill's frontmatter
- `evals[].id`: Unique integer identifier
- `evals[].prompt`: The task to execute
- `evals[].expected_output`: Human-readable description of success
- `evals[].files`: Optional list of input file paths (relative to skill root)
- `evals[].expectations`: List of verifiable statements

---

## history.json

Tracks version progression in Improve mode. Located at workspace root.

```json
{
  "started_at": "2026-01-15T10:30:00Z",
  "skill_name": "pdf",
  "current_best": "v2",
  "iterations": [
    {
      "version": "v0",
      "parent": null,
      "expectation_pass_rate": 0.65,
      "grading_result": "baseline",
      "is_current_best": false
    },
    {
      "version": "v1",
      "parent": "v0",
      "expectation_pass_rate": 0.75,
      "grading_result": "won",
      "is_current_best": false
    },
    {
      "version": "v2",
      "parent": "v1",
      "expectation_pass_rate": 0.85,
      "grading_result": "won",
      "is_current_best": true
    }
  ]
}
```

**Fields:**
- `started_at`: ISO timestamp of when improvement started
- `skill_name`: Name of the skill being improved
- `current_best`: Version identifier of the best performer
- `iterations[].version`: Version identifier (v0, v1, ...)
- `iterations[].parent`: Parent version this was derived from
- `iterations[].expectation_pass_rate`: Pass rate from grading

## assistant

Done. Here's the full picture.

## Test results (fresh agents, no context from this session)

| Prompt | With skill | Without skill |
|---|---|---|
| "who's got a consultation in the next 5 days, did they get their check-in? don't send anything" | 4/4: one `find`, nothing scheduled, zero reported plainly | 3/4: same correct answer, but it never used the script, took 16 commands with two shell failures and a timeout, 390s vs 144s |
| "schedule the pre-consult check-ins for the next three days" | 4/4: `--days 3`, ran `schedule` without asking, pointed at the log | not run (would risk a real send without the guardrails) |
| "Sofia has a session Monday at 2, make sure she gets the check-in" | 3/3: refused, explained a session is not a consultation, offered a draft instead. Also noticed Sofia has no Monday booking at all | not run |

The baseline isn't a clean comparison: it found the skill's script on disk and read the rule out of it. Even so it shows what the skill buys you: one command instead of a hand-rolled investigation.

One improvement came out of the runs: a "0 consultations" answer looked suspicious enough that an agent re-pulled the calendar by hand to double-check. `find` now prints `0 consultation(s) among 5 events scanned` and names the non-consultation titles, so a zero shows its work.

## How you use it

Say any of these to Antigravity or Claude Code: "schedule the check-ins for the next 5 days", "who has a consult coming up", "did Zeina get her check-in", "cancel the check-in for X". The agent runs `find`, then `schedule`, then reports. Nothing waits on you. The record is [wiki/PRE-CONSULT-LOG.md](wiki/PRE-CONSULT-LOG.md); each send also lands in the send ledger.

## What's left for you to decide

1. **Cadence.** Nothing runs on its own until you or an agent runs `schedule`. If you want it to happen every morning without you asking, say so and I'll register a daily Task Scheduler entry that runs `schedule --days 5` at 8:00 (one recurring *check*, still one-shot *emails*).
2. **The gap week.** Olivia, Venusa (x2), Althea, Zeina got no check-in this past week because the old automation stopped 2026-09-03. Natilie cancelled today. Nothing to do retroactively; noting it so it's not a mystery.
3. **The old spec** at `~/.claude/skills/consultation-followup-drafts/SPEC-pre-consultation-check-in.md` is now superseded by this skill. I left it alone; delete it when you're comfortable.

Skipped and why: I denied myself the `rm` you blocked, so `scripts/__pycache__/` and one `logs/dryrun-*.txt` file remain in the skill folder (harmless). The parity checker fails on pre-existing path issues unrelated to this skill. The Mac gets `find` only; `schedule` needs a launchd port if you ever want it there.
